Appendix

Appendix A
Preliminaries

In Section
A.1
we present some notation used in the Appendix. In Section
A.2
we introduce six auxiliary results needed in the proof of our main theorems (Theorems
1
,
2
) in Section
4
.

A.1
Notation

For
\displaystyle 0=t_{0}<\cdots<t_{n}
, with equi-distant step-size
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}
, we write
\displaystyle X_{{i}}
for the observation at time
\displaystyle t_{i}
of the solution of the hypo-elliptic SDE (
Hypo-II
) under the true parameter value
\displaystyle\theta^{\dagger}
, defined upon the filtered probability space
\displaystyle(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0},\mathbb{P})
. We denote by
\displaystyle\nu_{\theta^{\dagger}}
the invariant distribution of process (
Hypo-II
) under
\displaystyle\theta^{\dagger}
. In agreement with the structure of class (
Hypo-II
), we often represent
\displaystyle x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
and
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta\subset\mathbb{R}^{N_{\theta}}
as

\displaystyle\displaystyle x=(x_{S_{1}},x_{S_{2}},x_{R})\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{S_{1}}}\times\mathbb{R}^{N_{S_{2}}}\times\mathbb{R}^{N_{R}},\quad x_{S}\equiv(x_{S_{1}},x_{S_{2}});

\displaystyle\displaystyle\theta=(\beta_{S_{1}},\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta_{{\beta_{S_{1}}}}\times\Theta_{{\beta_{S_{2}}}}\times\Theta_{{\beta_{R}}}\times\Theta_{{\sigma}},\quad\beta_{S}\equiv(\beta_{S_{1}},\beta_{S_{2}}),

For
\displaystyle\varphi(\cdot,\theta):\mathbb{R}^{N}\to\mathbb{R}
,
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
, bounded up to second derivatives, we define the differential operators
\displaystyle\mathcal{L}
and
\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{j},\;1\leq j\leq d
:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\varphi(x,\theta)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}V_{0}^{i}(x,\theta)\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial x_{i}}(x,\theta)+\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{N}\sum_{k=1}^{d}V_{k}^{i_{1}}(x,\theta)V_{k}^{i_{2}}(x,\theta)\frac{\partial^{2}\varphi}{\partial x_{i_{1}}\partial x_{i_{2}}}(x,\theta);

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{j}\varphi(x,\theta)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}V_{j}^{i}(x,\theta)\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial x_{i}}(x,\theta),\quad 1\leq j\leq d.

Application of the above differential operators is extended to vector-valued functions in the apparent way, via separate consideration of each scalar component. We recall some notation used in the definition of the contrast function
\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\theta)
in (
13
).
We have that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mu(\Delta,x,\theta)=\bigl[\mu_{S_{1}}(\Delta,x,\theta)^{\top},\,\mu_{S_{2}}(\Delta,x,\theta)^{\top},\,\mu_{R}(\Delta,x,\theta)^{\top}\bigr]^{\top},

where

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\mu}_{S_{1}}(\Delta,x,\theta)\\[5.69046pt]
{\mu}_{S_{2}}(\Delta,x,\theta)\\[5.69046pt]
{\mu}_{R}(\Delta,x,\theta)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}x_{S_{1}}+V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\Delta+\mathcal{L}V_{S_{1},0}(x,\theta)\tfrac{\Delta^{2}}{2}+\mathcal{L}^{2}V_{S_{1},0}(x,\theta)\tfrac{\Delta^{3}}{6}\\[8.5359pt]
x_{S_{2}}+V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})\Delta+\mathcal{L}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\theta)\tfrac{\Delta^{2}}{2}\\[8.5359pt]
x_{R}+V_{R,0}(x,\beta_{R})\Delta\end{bmatrix}.

When
\displaystyle\Delta=1
, we simply write

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mu(x,\theta)\equiv\mu(1,x,\theta).

For
\displaystyle x=(x_{S_{1}},x_{S_{2}},x_{R})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\equiv\mathbb{R}^{N_{S_{1}}}\times\mathbb{R}^{N_{S_{2}}}\times\mathbb{R}^{N_{R}},\,y=(y_{S_{1}},y_{S_{2}},y_{R})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
,
\displaystyle\Delta>0
and
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
, we define

\displaystyle\displaystyle m(\Delta,x,y,\theta)=\left[\,\frac{y_{S_{1}}^{\top}-{\mu}_{S_{1}}(\Delta,x;\theta)^{\top}}{\sqrt{\Delta^{5}}},\;\frac{y_{S_{2}}^{\top}-{\mu}_{S_{2}}(\Delta,x;\theta)^{\top}}{\sqrt{\Delta^{3}}},\;\frac{y_{R}^{\top}-{\mu}_{R}(\Delta,x;\theta)^{\top}}{\sqrt{\Delta}}\,\right]^{\top}.

(27)

We write, for
\displaystyle 1\leq i\leq n
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle m_{i}(\Delta,\theta)\equiv m(\Delta,X_{{i-1}},X_{{i}},\theta).

(28)

We use
\displaystyle\Sigma(\Delta,x,\theta)
to represent the covariance of one step of the local Gaussian scheme (
LG-II
) for the hypo-elliptic SDE (
Hypo-II
), given step-size
\displaystyle\Delta>0
, initial point
\displaystyle x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
and parameter
\displaystyle\theta
.
We often write

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Sigma(x,\theta)\equiv\Sigma(1,x,\theta).

We express the inverse of
\displaystyle\Sigma(x,\theta)

as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Sigma^{-1}(x,\theta)=\Lambda(x,\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)&\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{2}}(x,\theta)&\Lambda_{S_{1}R}(x,\theta)\\[5.69046pt]
\Lambda_{S_{2}S_{1}}(x,\theta)&\Lambda_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)&\Lambda_{S_{2}R}(x,\theta)\\[5.69046pt]
\Lambda_{RS_{1}}(x,\theta)&\Lambda_{RS_{2}}(x,\theta)&\Lambda_{RR}(x,\theta)\end{bmatrix},

(29)

where each block matrix is specified as

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Lambda_{\iota_{1}\iota_{2}}(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{\iota_{1}}\times N_{\iota_{2}}},\quad\iota_{1},\iota_{2}\in\{S_{1},S_{2},R\}.

We emphasise here that
\displaystyle\Sigma(x,\theta)
and its inverse
\displaystyle\Lambda(x,\theta)
depend on
\displaystyle x
and
\displaystyle(\beta_{S},\sigma)
but not on the drift parameter
\displaystyle\beta_{R}
in the rough component, and this is critical in the proof of consistency of
\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{R,n}
. Thus, we sometimes write

\displaystyle\Sigma(x,(\beta_{S},\sigma))
and
\displaystyle\Lambda(x,(\beta_{S},\sigma))
to highlight the parameter dependency.
We recall the definition of the matrices

\displaystyle a_{R}(x,\sigma)\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{R}\times N_{R}}
,

\displaystyle a_{S_{1}}(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{S_{2}}\times N_{S_{2}}}
,

\displaystyle a_{S_{2}}(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{S_{1}}\times N_{S_{1}}}
as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle a_{R}(x,\sigma)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{d}V_{R,k}(x,\sigma)V_{R,k}(x,\sigma)^{\top},\quad a_{S_{2}}(x,\theta)=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\Bigl(\partial_{x_{R}}^{\top}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})V_{R,k}(x,\sigma)\Bigr)\Bigl(\partial_{x_{R}}^{\top}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})V_{R,k}(x,\sigma)\Bigr)^{\top};

\displaystyle\displaystyle a_{S_{1}}(x,\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\,\Bigl(\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\partial_{x_{R}}^{\top}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})V_{R,k}(x,\sigma)\Bigr)\Bigl(\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\partial_{x_{R}}^{\top}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})V_{R,k}(x,\sigma)\Bigr)^{\top}.

We define the mappings

\displaystyle\displaystyle\eta_{S_{1}}:\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta_{\beta_{S_{1}}}\to\mathbb{R}^{N_{S_{1}}},\quad\eta_{S_{2}}:\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta_{\beta_{S_{2}}}\to\mathbb{R}^{N_{S_{2}}},\quad\eta_{R}:\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta_{\beta_{R}}\to\mathbb{R}^{N_{R}}

as

\displaystyle\displaystyle\eta_{S_{1}}(x,\beta_{S_{1}})=V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}})-V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}}),\quad\eta_{S_{2}}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})=V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{2}})-V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}});

\displaystyle\displaystyle\eta_{R}(x,\beta_{R})=V_{R,0}(x,\beta^{\dagger}_{R})-V_{R,0}(x,\beta_{R}).

We write, with a slight abuse of notation, for
\displaystyle 0\leq i\leq n
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\eta_{S_{1},i}(\Delta,\beta_{S_{1}})\equiv\tfrac{\eta_{S_{1}}(X_{{i}},\,\beta_{S_{1}})}{\Delta},\quad\eta_{S_{2},i}(\Delta,\beta_{S_{2}})\equiv\tfrac{\eta_{S_{2}}(X_{{i}},{\beta}_{S_{2}})}{\Delta}.

We denote by
\displaystyle\mathcal{S}
the space of functions
\displaystyle f:[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}
so that there are constants
\displaystyle C,q>0
such that

\displaystyle|f(\Delta,x,\theta)|\leq C\Delta\,(1+|x|^{q})
for any
\displaystyle(\Delta,x,\theta)\in[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta
. For an
\displaystyle M_{1}\times M_{2}
matrix
\displaystyle A
, with
\displaystyle M_{1},M_{2}\geq 1
, we write each matrix entry as
\displaystyle[A]_{ij}
for
\displaystyle 1\leq i\leq M_{1},\,1\leq j\leq M_{2}
.
An expectation under the probability law
\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta}
is written as
\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta}
.
We write
\displaystyle\textstyle\partial_{u}=\big[{\partial}/{\partial u_{1}},\ldots,{\partial}/{\partial u_{n}}\big]^{\top},\,\partial^{2}_{u}=\partial_{u}\partial_{u}^{\top}\equiv\big({\partial^{2}}/{\partial u_{i}\partial u_{j}}\big)_{i,j=1}^{n}
for the standard differential operators acting upon maps
\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
,
\displaystyle n\geq 1
.
We also write
\displaystyle\partial^{u}_{\alpha}={\partial^{l}}/{\partial u_{\alpha_{1}}\cdots\partial u_{\alpha_{l}}}
for a multi-index
\displaystyle\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,n\}^{l}
,
\displaystyle l\in\mathbb{N}
. For a function
\displaystyle g:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{m},\,n,m\in\mathbb{N}
, we write:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\partial_{u}g(u)^{\top}=\bigl[\tfrac{\partial}{\partial u_{i}}g^{j}(u)\bigr]_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq n,\,1\leq j\leq m\end{subarray}},\quad\partial_{u}^{\top}g(u)=\bigl(\partial_{u}g(u)^{\top}\bigr)^{\top};

\displaystyle\displaystyle\partial^{u}_{\alpha}g(u)^{\top}=\bigl[\partial^{u}_{\alpha}g^{1}(u),\ldots,\partial^{u}_{\alpha}g^{m}(u)\bigr],\,\quad\partial^{u}_{\alpha}g(u)=\bigl(\partial^{u}_{\alpha}g(u)^{\top}\bigr)^{\top}.

A.2
Auxiliary Results

Lemma 1
.

Let
\displaystyle Y_{t_{i}}
,
\displaystyle U
be random variables, with
\displaystyle Y_{t_{i}}
being
\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{t_{i}}
-measurable. If

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\theta^{\dagger}}[\,Y_{t_{i}}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{t_{i-1}}\,]\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}U,\quad\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\theta^{\dagger}}\big[\,(Y_{t_{i}})^{2}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{t_{i-1}}\,\big]\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}0,

then
\displaystyle\textstyle{\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{t_{i}}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}U}
.

Proof.

See Lemma 9 in
[
13
]
.
∎

Lemma 2
.

Let
\displaystyle f:\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}
be differentiable w.r.t.
\displaystyle(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta
with derivatives of polynomial growth in
\displaystyle x
uniformly in
\displaystyle\theta
. Under conditions (
C1
)–(
C4
), it holds that, if
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, then

\displaystyle\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\int f(x,\theta)\nu_{\theta^{\dagger}}(dx),

uniformly in
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
.

Proof.

This is a multivariate version of Lemma 8 in
[
21
]
, so we omit the proof.
∎

Lemma 3
.

Let
\displaystyle 1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq N
and assume that
\displaystyle f:\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}
is as in Lemma
2
. Under conditions (
C1
)–(
C4
), it holds that, if
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, then

\displaystyle\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\,m^{j_{1}}_{i}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger})\,m^{j_{2}}_{i}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\int f(x,\theta)\bigl[{\Sigma}(x,\theta^{\dagger})\bigr]_{j_{1}j_{2}}\nu_{\theta^{\dagger}}(dx);

(30)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{n\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(X_{{i-1}},\theta)m^{j_{1}}_{i}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}0,

(31)

uniformly in
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
.

Proof.

Notice that for any
\displaystyle 1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq N
and
\displaystyle 0\leq i\leq n
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta^{\dagger}}\bigl[m^{j_{1}}_{i}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger})\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{t_{i-1}}\bigr]=R_{j_{1}}(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}},X_{{i-1}},\theta^{\dagger});

(32)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\theta^{\dagger}}\bigl[m^{j_{1}}_{i}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger})\,m^{j_{2}}_{i}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger})\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{t_{i-1}}\bigr]=\bigl[\Sigma(X_{{i-1}},\theta^{\dagger})\bigr]_{j_{1}j_{2}}+R_{j_{1}j_{2}}(\Delta_{n},X_{{i-1}},\theta^{\dagger}),

(33)

where
\displaystyle R_{j_{1}j_{2}},\,R_{j_{1}}\in\mathcal{S}
. Applying Lemmas
1
,
2
with formulae (
32
) and (
33
), we obtain (
30
) and (
31
) in the same way as in the proof of Lemma 12 in
[
18
]
.
∎

Lemma 4
.

Assume that condition
(H)
-II holds. We have that for any
\displaystyle(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta
:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)\,\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})=-2\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{2}}(x,\theta).

Proof.

We write
\displaystyle\Sigma(x,\theta)
,
\displaystyle(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta
, in the form of the block matrix:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Sigma(x,\theta)=\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)&\widetilde{\Sigma}(x,\theta)\\[5.69046pt]
\widetilde{\Sigma}(x,\theta)^{\top}&\hat{\Sigma}(x,\theta)\end{bmatrix},

(34)

where we have set:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\widetilde{\Sigma}(x,\theta)=\Bigl[\Sigma_{S_{1}S_{2}}(x,\theta),\,\Sigma_{S_{1}R}(x,\theta)\Bigr],\quad\hat{\Sigma}(x,\theta)=\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)&\Sigma_{S_{2}R}(x,\theta)\\[5.69046pt]
\Sigma_{RS_{2}}(x,\theta)&\Sigma_{RR}(x,\theta)\end{bmatrix}.

(35)

Notice that under condition
(H)
–II, matrix
\displaystyle\hat{\Sigma}(x,\theta)
is invertible for any
\displaystyle(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta
. We write the inverse of
\displaystyle\hat{\Sigma}(x,\theta)
as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hat{\Sigma}^{-1}(x,\theta)=\hat{\Lambda}(x,\theta)=\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\Lambda}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)&\hat{\Lambda}_{S_{2}R}(x,\theta)\\[5.69046pt]
\hat{\Lambda}_{RS_{2}}(x,\theta)&\hat{\Lambda}_{RR}(x,\theta)\end{bmatrix}.

Recall the notation for the inverse of
\displaystyle\Sigma(x,\theta)
in (
29
). Using the inverse formula for a block matrix, we obtain:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{2}}(x,\theta)=-\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)\Xi(x,\theta),

(36)

where we have set

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Xi(x,\theta)=\Sigma_{S_{1}S_{2}}(x,\theta)\hat{\Lambda}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)+\Sigma_{S_{1}R}(x,\theta)\hat{\Lambda}_{RS_{2}}(x,\theta).

(37)

From the block matrix representation of
\displaystyle\Sigma(\Delta,x,\theta)
in (
10
), we obtain

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Sigma_{S_{1}S_{2}}(x,\theta)=\tfrac{3}{8}\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\Sigma_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta),\quad\Sigma_{S_{1}R}(x,\theta)=\tfrac{1}{3}\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\Sigma_{S_{2}R}(x,\theta).

We then have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Xi(x,\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{3}{8}\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\Sigma_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)\hat{\Lambda}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)+\tfrac{1}{3}\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\Sigma_{S_{2}R}(x,\theta)\hat{\Lambda}_{RS_{2}}(x,\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{24}\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\Sigma_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)\hat{\Lambda}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)+\tfrac{1}{3}\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}}).

(38)

In the above calculation we have used:

\displaystyle\displaystyle{\Sigma}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)\hat{\Lambda}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)+{\Sigma}_{S_{2}R}(x,\theta)\hat{\Lambda}_{RS_{2}}(x,\theta)=I_{N_{S_{2}}\times N_{S_{2}}};

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hat{\Lambda}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)=\bigl({\Sigma}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)-\Sigma_{S_{2}R}(x,\theta)\Sigma^{-1}_{RR}(x,\theta)\Sigma_{RS_{2}}(x,\theta)\bigr)^{-1}=4{\Sigma}_{S_{2}S_{2}}^{-1}(x,\theta),

where matrix
\displaystyle\Sigma_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)
is invertible under condition
(H)
–II.
Thus, from (
36
) and (
38
), we obtain

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{2}}(x,\theta)=-\tfrac{1}{2}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}}),

(39)

and the proof is now complete.
∎

Lemma 5
.

Assume that condition
(H)
-II holds. We have that, for any
\displaystyle(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta
:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)=720\,a_{S_{1}}^{-1}(x,\theta);

(40)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Lambda_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)=12a_{S_{2}}^{-1}(x,\theta)-\tfrac{1}{2}\Lambda_{S_{2}S_{1}}(x,\theta)\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}}).

(41)

Proof.

(
Proof of (
40
)
).
First, we note that the matrices

\displaystyle\Sigma_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta),\,\Sigma_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta),\,\Sigma_{RR}(x,\theta)

are invertible for any
\displaystyle(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta
under condition
(H)
-II. Due to the block expression of matrix
\displaystyle\Sigma(x,\theta)
in (
34
), we have:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)=\Bigl(\Sigma_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)-\widetilde{\Sigma}(x,\theta)\hat{\Lambda}(x,\theta)\widetilde{\Sigma}(x,\theta)^{\top}\Bigr)^{-1},

where
\displaystyle\hat{\Lambda}(x,\theta)
, the inverse of matrix
\displaystyle\hat{\Sigma}(x,\theta)
given in (
35
), has the following block expression:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hat{\Lambda}(x,\theta)=\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\Lambda}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)&\hat{\Lambda}_{S_{2}R}(x,\theta)\\[2.84544pt]
\hat{\Lambda}_{S_{2}R}(x,\theta)^{\top}&\hat{\Lambda}_{RR}(x,\theta)\end{bmatrix}

where we have set:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hat{\Lambda}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)=4\Sigma_{S_{2}S_{2}}^{-1}(x,\theta),\quad\hat{\Lambda}_{S_{2}R}(x,\theta)=-4\Sigma^{-1}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)\Sigma_{S_{2}R}(x,\theta)\Sigma_{RR}^{-1}(x,\sigma);

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hat{\Lambda}_{RR}(x,\theta)=\Sigma_{RR}^{-1}(x,\sigma)+4\Sigma_{RR}^{-1}(x,\sigma)\Sigma_{RS_{2}}(x,\theta)\Sigma^{-1}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)\Sigma_{S_{2}R}(x,\theta)\Sigma_{RR}^{-1}(x,\sigma).

We then have:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hat{\Lambda}(x,\theta)\widetilde{\Sigma}(x,\theta)^{\top}=\begin{bmatrix}\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl(\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\bigr)^{\top}\\[2.84544pt]
-\tfrac{1}{12}\bigl(\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\partial_{x_{R}}^{\top}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})\bigr)^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

where we used (
38
) for the upper block matrix, while the lower one is obtained via:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hat{\Lambda}_{S_{2}R}(x,\theta)^{\top}\Sigma_{S_{2}S_{1}}(x,\theta)+\hat{\Lambda}_{RR}(x,\theta)\Sigma_{RS_{1}}(x,\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=-4\Sigma_{RR}^{-1}(x,\sigma)\Sigma_{RS_{2}}(x,\theta)\Sigma^{-1}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)\Sigma_{S_{2}S_{1}}(x,\theta)+\Sigma_{RR}^{-1}(x,\sigma)\Sigma_{RS_{1}}(x,\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+4\Sigma_{RR}^{-1}(x,\sigma)\Sigma_{RS_{2}}(x,\theta)\Sigma^{-1}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)\Sigma_{S_{2}R}(x,\theta)\Sigma_{RR}^{-1}(x,\sigma)\Sigma_{RS_{1}}(x,\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=-\tfrac{3}{4}\bigl(\partial_{x_{R}}^{\top}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})\bigr)^{\top}\bigl(\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\bigr)^{\top}+\tfrac{1}{6}\bigl(\partial_{x_{R}}^{\top}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})\bigr)^{\top}\bigl(\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\bigr)^{\top}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl(\partial_{x_{R}}^{\top}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})\bigr)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)\Sigma_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)\bigl(\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\bigr)^{\top}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=-\tfrac{1}{12}\bigl(\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\partial_{x_{R}}^{\top}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})\bigr)^{\top}.

Thus, we obtain:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Bigl(\Sigma_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)-\widetilde{\Sigma}(x,\theta)\hat{\Lambda}(x,\theta)\widetilde{\Sigma}(x,\theta)^{\top}\Bigr)^{-1}=\bigl(\tfrac{1}{36}\Sigma_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)\bigr)^{-1}=720\,a_{S_{1}}^{-1}(x,\theta),

and now the proof of (
40
) is complete.

(
Proof of (
41
)
). From the block expression of the matrix
\displaystyle\Sigma(x,\theta)
in (
34
), we obtain:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\Lambda_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)&\Lambda_{S_{2}R}(x,\theta)\\[2.84544pt]
\Lambda_{RS_{2}}(x,\theta)&\Lambda_{RR}(x,\theta)\end{bmatrix}=\hat{\Lambda}(x,\theta)+\hat{\Lambda}(x,\theta)\widetilde{\Sigma}(x,\theta)^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)\widetilde{\Sigma}(x,\theta)\hat{\Lambda}(x,\theta).

Thus, we have:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Lambda_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\hat{\Lambda}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)+\Xi(x,\theta)^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)\Xi(x,\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=4\Sigma^{-1}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)+\tfrac{1}{4}\bigl(\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\bigr)^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})

\displaystyle\displaystyle=12a_{S_{2}}^{-1}(x,\theta)-\tfrac{1}{2}\Lambda_{S_{2}S_{1}}(x,\theta)\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}}),

where
\displaystyle\Xi(x,\theta)
is defined as in (

), and we made use of (

), (

). The proof of (

Lemma 6
.

Assume that conditions
(H)
-II and (
C1
) hold. For any
\displaystyle\theta=(\beta_{S},\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta
and
\displaystyle 1\leq i\leq N
, we have that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Lambda(X_{{i-1}},(\beta_{S},\sigma))\bigl(m_{i}(\Delta_{n},\theta)-m_{i}(\Delta_{n},(\beta_{S},\beta^{\dagger}_{R},\sigma))\bigr)=\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{N_{S_{1}}}\\[2.84544pt]
\mathbf{0}_{N_{S_{2}}}\\[2.84544pt]
b_{R}\end{bmatrix}+R(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}},X_{{i-1}},\theta),

for
\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{N}
-valued function
\displaystyle R
with
\displaystyle R^{j}\in\mathcal{S},\,1\leq j\leq N
, where the
\displaystyle N_{R}
-dimensional vector
\displaystyle b_{R}
is specified as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle b_{R}\equiv a_{R}^{-1}(X_{{i-1}},\sigma)\,\eta_{R}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{R}).

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\displaystyle m_{i}(\Delta_{n},\theta)-m_{i}(\Delta_{n},(\beta_{S},\beta^{\dagger}_{R},\sigma))

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\tfrac{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}{6}\,\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{S,{i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}})\partial_{x_{R}}^{\top}V_{S_{2},0}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{2}})\,\eta_{R}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{R})\\[5.69046pt]
\tfrac{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}{2}\,\partial_{x_{R}}^{\top}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})\,\eta_{R}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{R})\\[5.69046pt]
\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}\,\eta_{R}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{R})\end{bmatrix}+R(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}},X_{{i-1}},\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{S_{1}R}\bigl(X_{{i-1}},\theta\bigr)\\[5.69046pt]
\Sigma_{S_{2}R}\bigl(X_{{i-1}},\theta\bigr)\\[5.69046pt]
\Sigma_{RR}\bigl(X_{{i-1}},\sigma\bigr)\end{bmatrix}a_{R}^{-1}(X_{{i-1}},\sigma)\eta_{R}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{R})+R(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}},X_{{i-1}},\theta),

for
\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{N}
-valued function
\displaystyle R
with
\displaystyle R^{j}\in\mathcal{S},\,1\leq j\leq N
, where we used for
\displaystyle(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{L}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\partial_{x_{R}}^{\top}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})V_{R,0}(x,\beta_{R})+v_{S_{2}}(x,\beta_{S},\sigma);

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{2}V_{S_{1},0}(x,\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\partial_{x_{R}}^{\top}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})V_{R,0}(x,\beta_{R})+v_{S_{1}}(x,\beta_{S},\sigma),

with some functions
\displaystyle v_{S_{2}}:\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta_{\beta_{S}}\times\Theta_{\sigma}\to\mathbb{R}^{N_{S_{2}}}

and
\displaystyle v_{S_{1}}:\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta_{\beta_{S}}\times\Theta_{\sigma}\to\mathbb{R}^{N_{S_{1}}}
that are independent of
\displaystyle\beta_{R}\in\Theta_{\beta_{R}}
.
Thus, it follows that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Lambda\bigl(X_{{i-1}},(\beta_{S},\sigma)\bigr)\bigl(m_{i}(\Delta_{n},\theta)-m_{i}(\Delta_{n},(\beta_{S},\beta^{\dagger}_{R},\sigma))\bigr)=\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}\begin{bmatrix}b_{S_{1}}\\
b_{S_{2}}\\
b_{R}\end{bmatrix}a_{R}^{-1}(X_{{i-1}},\sigma)\eta_{R}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{R})+\widetilde{R}(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}},X_{{i-1}},\theta),

for
\displaystyle\widetilde{R}^{j}\in\mathcal{S},\,1\leq j\leq N
, where we have set:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}b_{S_{1}}\\[2.84544pt]
b_{S_{2}}\\[2.84544pt]
b_{R}\end{bmatrix}=\Lambda\big(X_{{i-1}},\theta\bigr)\cdot\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{S_{1}R}\bigl(X_{{i-1}},\theta\bigr)\\[5.69046pt]
\Sigma_{S_{2}R}\bigl(X_{{i-1}},\theta\bigr)\\[5.69046pt]
\Sigma_{RR}\bigl(X_{{i-1}},\sigma\bigr)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{N_{S_{1}}\times N_{R}}\\[2.84544pt]
\mathbf{0}_{N_{S_{2}}\times N_{R}}\\[2.84544pt]
I_{N_{R}\times N_{R}}\end{bmatrix},

since it holds
\displaystyle\Lambda(x,\theta)\Sigma(x,\theta)=I_{N\times N}
for each
\displaystyle(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta
.
The proof is now complete.
∎

Appendix B
Proof of Proposition
1

The first equation within condition
(H)
-II immediately leads to:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\inf_{(x,\sigma)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta}|a_{R}(x,\sigma)|>0.

Furthermore, the first and second equations of condition
(H)
-II yield:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\inf_{(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta}\ \inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{S_{2}}}\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\,\|\xi\|=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\Bigl\langle\mathrm{proj}_{N_{S_{1}}+1,N_{S_{1}}+N_{S_{2}}}\bigl\{\bigl[\widetilde{V}_{0},V_{k}\bigr](x,\theta)\bigr\},\ \xi\Bigr\rangle^{2}>0.

Noticing that
\displaystyle\mathrm{proj}_{N_{S_{1}}+1,N_{S_{1}}+N_{S_{2}}}\bigl\{\bigl[\widetilde{V}_{0},V_{k}\bigr](x,\theta)\bigr\}=\partial_{x_{R}}^{\top}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})V_{R,k}(x,\sigma)
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\inf_{(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta}|a_{S_{2}}(x,\theta)|>0.

Similarly, under condition
(H)
-II,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\inf_{(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta}\ \inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{S_{1}}}\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\,\|\xi\|=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\Bigl\langle\mathrm{proj}_{1,N_{S_{1}}}\bigl\{\bigl[\widetilde{V}_{0},[\widetilde{V}_{0},V_{k}]\bigr](x,\theta)\bigr\},\ \xi\Bigr\rangle^{2}>0.

Since
\displaystyle\mathrm{proj}_{1,N_{S_{1}}}\bigl\{\bigl[\widetilde{V}_{0},[\widetilde{V}_{0},V_{k}]\bigr](x,\theta)\bigr\}=\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\partial_{x_{R}}^{\top}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})V_{R,k}(x,\sigma)
, we have that:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\inf_{(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta_{\sigma}}|a_{S_{1}}(x,\theta)|>0.

Finally, from the expression of the covariance
\displaystyle\Sigma(\Delta,x,\theta)
in (
10
), its determinant is given as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle|\Sigma(\Delta,x,\theta)|=\tfrac{\Delta^{9}}{8640}\,|a_{R}(x,\sigma)|\,|a_{S_{1}}(x,\theta)|\,|a_{S_{2}}(x,\theta)|.

Thus, (
12
) holds and the proof is complete.

Appendix C
Proof of Main Results

In this section
we prove the main results, i.e. Theorem
1
,
2
in Section
4
of the main text. The proofs make use of some technical results from Appendix
D
.

C.1
Proof of Theorem
1
– Consistency

To show consistency, we study the limit of the contrast function
\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\theta)
, defined in (
13
), that involves terms such as

\displaystyle\displaystyle\tfrac{X_{S_{1},i+1}-\mu_{S_{1}}(\Delta_{n},X_{i},\theta)}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{5}}},\ \ \tfrac{X_{S_{2},{i+1}}-\mu_{S_{2}}(\Delta_{n},X_{i},\theta)}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}}},\ \ 1\leq i\leq n-1,

where
\displaystyle\{X_{i}\}_{i=0,\ldots,n}
are discrete-time observations under the true model (
Hypo-II
) with parameter
\displaystyle\theta^{\dagger}
. Then, the stochastic Taylor expansion for
\displaystyle X_{S,{i+1}}
yields

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tfrac{X_{S_{1},i+1}-\mu_{S_{1}}(\Delta_{n},X_{i},\theta)}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{5}}}&=\tfrac{V_{S_{1},0}(X_{S,i},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}})-V_{S_{1},0}(X_{S,i},\beta_{S_{1}})}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}}}+R_{S_{1}}(\Delta_{n},X_{i},\theta);\\[5.69046pt]
\tfrac{X_{S_{2},{i+1}}-\mu_{S_{2}}(\Delta_{n},X_{i},\theta)}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}}}&=\tfrac{V_{S_{2},0}(X_{i},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{2}})-V_{S_{2},0}(X_{i},\beta_{S_{2}})}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}+R_{S_{2}}(\Delta_{n},X_{i},\theta),\end{aligned}

(42)

where
\displaystyle R_{S_{1}}^{j_{1}},\,R_{S_{2}}^{j_{2}}\in\mathcal{S}
for
\displaystyle 1\leq j_{1}\leq N_{S_{1}},\,1\leq j_{2}\leq N_{S_{2}}
. Careful steps are needed to control the first terms in the right-hand sides of (
42
) as
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
, within the proof of consistency. Our proof proceeds with the following strategy which extends arguments used in
[
18
]
:

Step 1.

We prove consistency, along with a convergence rate, for the estimator
\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}
. That is, if
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, then

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\beta_{S_{1}}^{\dagger}.

In particular, we show the rate:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}}}\bigl(\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}-\beta_{S_{1}}^{\dagger}\bigr)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}0.

(43)

Step 2.

Making use of the convergence rate in (
43
), we prove consistency, along with a convergence rate, for the estimator
\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{S_{2},n}
. That is, if
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, then
\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{S_{2},n}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\beta_{S_{2}}^{\dagger}
. In particular, we show the rate:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\bigl(\hat{\beta}_{S_{2},n}-\beta_{S_{2}}^{\dagger}\bigr)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}0.

(44)

Step 3.

Making use of the rates in (
43
) and (
44
), we prove consistency for the estimators
\displaystyle(\hat{\beta}_{R,n},\hat{\sigma}_{n})
. That is, if
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, then
\displaystyle(\hat{\beta}_{R,n},\hat{\sigma}_{n})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}(\beta^{\dagger}_{R},\sigma^{\dagger})
.

We emphasise that in our proof of consistency, the condition
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}=o(n^{-1/2})
is not required
while
[
14
]
assumed the condition throughout the proof of consistency in the case of the degenerate diffusion class (
Hypo-I
). Typically, in order to show the consistency of
\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{R,n}
,
[
14
]
exploited the rates of convergence

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sqrt{\tfrac{n}{\Delta_{n}}}(\beta_{S,n}-\beta^{\dagger}_{S})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}0,\quad\sqrt{n}(\sigma_{n}-\sigma^{\dagger})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}0

that are derived under the condition
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}=o(n^{-1/2})
. In contrast, in our strategy, the rates of convergence (
43
) and (
44
) are obtained without requiring
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}=o(n^{-1/2})
, and are put into effective use to avoid explosion of terms such as

\displaystyle\displaystyle\tfrac{V_{S_{1},0}(X_{S,{i-1}},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}})-V_{S_{1},0}(X_{S,{i-1}},\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n})}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}}},\quad\tfrac{V_{S_{2},0}(X_{i},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{2}})-V_{S_{2},0}(X_{i},\hat{\beta}_{S_{2},n})}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}

as
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
only, with the help of some results derived from straightforward matrix calculations, i.e., Lemmas
4
,
5
and
6
in Appendix
A.2
.

Remark 8
.

Due to (
11
) in Proposition
1
, we have under conditions
(H)
-II and (
C1
) that:

\displaystyle\displaystyle a_{S_{1},i_{1}j_{1}}^{-1}\in C_{p}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta;\mathbb{R}),\quad a_{S_{2},i_{2}j_{2}}^{-1}\in C_{p}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta;\mathbb{R}),\quad a_{R,i_{3}j_{3}}^{-1}\in C_{p}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta;\mathbb{R}),

for
\displaystyle 1\leq i_{1},j_{1}\leq N_{S_{1}}
,
\displaystyle 1\leq i_{2},j_{2}\leq N_{S_{2}}
, and
\displaystyle 1\leq i_{3},j_{3}\leq N_{R}
. Thus, one is able to apply Lemmas
2
,
3
for the (scaled) contrast function that includes the above inverse matrices in the proofs below.

C.1.1
Step 1

Consistency of the estimator
\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}
is deduced from the following result.

Lemma 7
.

Assume that conditions
(H)
-II and (
C1
)–(
C4
) hold. If
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, then,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\tfrac{\Delta_{n}^{3}}{n}\ell_{n}(\theta)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\int\eta_{S_{1}}(x,\beta_{S_{1}})^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)\,\eta_{S_{1}}(x,\beta_{S_{1}})\nu_{\theta^{\dagger}}(dx),

(45)

uniformly in
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
.

The proof is given in Appendix
D.1
. Lemma
7
implies the consistency of
\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}
via the following discussion. We write the right-hand side of (
45
) as
\displaystyle\mathscr{Q}(\theta),\,\theta=(\beta_{S_{1}},\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta
. Under condition
(H)
-II, it follows from Proposition
1
and (
40
) in Lemma
5
that for all non-zero
\displaystyle\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{S_{1}}}
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\inf_{(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta}\,\xi^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)\,\xi>0.

Thus, it holds under the identifiablity condition (
C5
) that for any
\displaystyle\varepsilon>0
there exists a constant
\displaystyle\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}\Bigl(\,\bigl|\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}-\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}}\bigr|>\varepsilon\Bigr)\leq\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}\Bigl(\,\bigl|\mathscr{Q}(\hat{\theta}_{n})\bigr|>\delta\Bigr).

Then, Lemma
7
together with the definition of the estimator
\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{n}
yields:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}\Bigl(\,\bigl|\mathscr{Q}(\hat{\theta}_{n})\bigr|>\delta\Bigr)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}\Bigl(\,\bigl|\tfrac{\Delta_{n}^{3}}{n}\ell_{n}\bigl(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},\hat{\beta}_{S_{2},n},\hat{\beta}_{R,n},\hat{\sigma}_{n}\bigr)-\tfrac{\Delta_{n}^{3}}{n}\ell_{n}\bigl(\hat{\theta}_{n}\bigr)+\mathscr{Q}(\hat{\theta}_{n})\bigr|>\delta\Bigr)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}\Bigl(\,\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\,\bigl|\tfrac{\Delta_{n}^{3}}{n}\ell_{n}\bigl(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},{\beta}_{S_{2}},{\beta}_{R},\sigma_{n}\bigr)-\tfrac{\Delta_{n}^{3}}{n}\ell_{n}\bigl(\theta\bigr)+\mathscr{Q}(\theta)\bigr|>\delta\Bigr)\to 0

as
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, which leads to the consistency of
\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}
.

We now prove the rate of convergence in (
43
). Considering the Taylor expansion of
\displaystyle\partial_{\beta_{S_{1}}}\ell_{n}(\hat{\theta}_{n})
around the derivative

\displaystyle\partial_{\beta_{S_{1}}}\ell_{n}(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},\hat{\beta}_{S_{2},n},\hat{\beta}_{R,n},\hat{\sigma}_{n})
with an appropriate scaling factor, we obtain

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{S_{1},n}(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},\hat{\beta}_{S_{2},n},\hat{\beta}_{R,n},\hat{\sigma}_{n})=\mathscr{B}_{S_{1},n}(\hat{\theta}_{n})\;\times\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}}}(\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}-\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}}),

where we have set, for

\displaystyle\theta=(\beta_{S_{1}},\theta^{-\beta_{S_{1}}})\in\Theta
with
\displaystyle\theta^{-\beta_{S_{1}}}\equiv(\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma)
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{S_{1},n}(\theta)=-\tfrac{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}}}{n}\partial_{\beta_{S_{1}}}\ell_{n}(\theta),\quad\mathscr{B}_{S_{1},n}(\theta)=\tfrac{\Delta_{n}^{3}}{n}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\beta_{S_{1}}}^{2}\ell_{n}\bigl(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}}+\lambda({\beta}_{S_{1}}-\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}}),\theta^{-\beta_{S_{1}}}\bigr)\,d\lambda.

For the matrix
\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{S_{1},n}(\theta)
, we have the following result:

Lemma 8
.

Assume that conditions
(H)
-II and (
C1
)–(
C5
) hold.
If
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, then

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{S_{1},n}(\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n},\theta^{-\beta_{S_{1}}})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\int\partial_{\beta_{S_{1}}}\bigl(V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}})\bigr)^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}\bigl(x,(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},\theta^{-\beta_{S_{1}}})\bigr)\partial_{\beta_{S_{1}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}})\,\nu_{\theta^{\dagger}}(dx),

uniformly in
\displaystyle\theta^{-\beta_{S_{1}}}\equiv(\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta_{\beta_{S_{2}}}\times\Theta_{\beta_{R}}\times\Theta_{\sigma}
.

We give the proof in Appendix
D.2
. We now check that
\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{S_{1},n}(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},\theta^{-\beta_{S_{1}}})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\mathbf{0}_{N_{\beta_{S_{1}}}}
as
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, uniformly in
\displaystyle\theta^{-\beta_{S_{1}}}=(\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma)
.
We have:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{S_{1},n}(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},\theta^{-\beta_{S_{1}}})=\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}_{S_{1},n}(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},\theta^{-\beta_{S_{1}}})+\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}R\big(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}},X_{{i-1}},(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},\theta^{-\beta_{S_{1}}})\big),

for
\displaystyle R^{j}\in\mathcal{S},\,1\leq j\leq N_{\beta_{S_{1}}}
, where we have set, for
\displaystyle\theta=(\beta_{S_{1}},\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}_{S_{1},n}(\theta)=\tfrac{1}{n\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\partial_{\beta_{S_{1}}}\bigl(V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{S,i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}})\bigr)^{\top}\,\Phi(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\,\eta_{S_{2}}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{2}})

with
\displaystyle\Phi:\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}^{N_{S_{1}}\times N_{S_{2}}}
defined as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Phi(x,\theta)=\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)\,\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})+2\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{2}}(x,\theta).

(46)

From Lemmas
2
and
3
in Appendix
A
, we immediately have that if
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, then

\displaystyle\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}R\bigl(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}},X_{{i-1}},(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},\,\theta^{-\beta_{S_{1}}})\bigr)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\mathbf{0}_{N_{\beta_{S_{1}}}},

uniformly in
\displaystyle\theta^{-\beta_{S_{1}}}
. Furthermore, we have from Lemma
4
that, for any
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
,
\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}_{S_{1},n}(\theta)=\mathbf{0}_{N_{\beta_{S_{1}}}}
with probability 1, since
\displaystyle\Phi(x,\theta)=\mathbf{0}_{N_{S_{1}}\times N_{S_{2}}}
for any
\displaystyle(x,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta
. Hence, we obtain
\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{S_{1},n}(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},\theta^{-\beta_{S_{1}}})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\mathbf{0}_{N_{\beta_{S_{1}}}}
,
and now convergence (
43
) holds.

C.1.2
Step 2

Making use of convergence (
43
), we obtain the following result whose proof is postponed to Appendix
D.3
.

Lemma 9
.

Assume that conditions
(H)
-II and (
C1
)–(
C5
) hold.
If
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, then

\displaystyle\displaystyle\tfrac{\Delta_{n}}{n}\ell_{n}(\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n},\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}12\times\int\eta_{S_{2}}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})^{\top}a_{S_{2}}^{-1}\bigl(x,(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},\beta_{S_{2}},\sigma)\bigr)\,\eta_{S_{2}}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})\,\nu_{\theta^{\dagger}}(dx),

uniformly in
\displaystyle(\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta_{\beta_{S_{2}}}\times\Theta_{\beta_{R}}\times\Theta_{\sigma}
.

This result leads to the consistency of
\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{S_{2},n}
following an argument similar to the one used in
Step 1
to show consistency of
\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}
.

To prove convergence (
44
), we apply a Taylor expansion on the contrast function to get:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{S,n}(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{2}},\hat{\beta}_{R,n},\hat{\sigma}_{n})=\mathscr{B}_{S,n}(\hat{\theta}_{n})\;\times\begin{bmatrix}\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}}}(\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}-\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}})\\[8.5359pt]
\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}(\hat{\beta}_{S_{2},n}-\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{2}})\end{bmatrix},

where we have set, for
\displaystyle\theta=(\beta_{S},\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta
with
\displaystyle\beta_{S}\equiv(\beta_{S_{1}},\beta_{S_{2}})
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{S,n}(\theta)=\begin{bmatrix}-\tfrac{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}}}{n}\partial_{\beta_{S_{1}}}\ell_{n}(\theta)\\[5.69046pt]
-\tfrac{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}{n}\partial_{\beta_{S_{2}}}\ell_{n}(\theta)\end{bmatrix},\qquad\mathscr{B}_{S,n}\bigl(\theta\bigr)=\int_{0}^{1}M_{\beta_{S},n}\,\partial_{\beta_{S}}^{2}\ell_{n}\bigl(\beta^{\dagger}_{S}+\lambda(\beta_{S}-\beta^{\dagger}_{S}),\beta_{R},\sigma\bigr)\,M_{\beta_{S},n}d\lambda,

and
\displaystyle M_{\beta_{S},n}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{\beta_{S}}\times N_{\beta_{S}}}
is defined as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle M_{\beta_{S},n}=\mathrm{diag}\Bigl(\Bigl[\,\underbrace{\sqrt{\tfrac{\Delta_{n}^{3}}{n}},\ldots,\sqrt{\tfrac{\Delta_{n}^{3}}{n}}}_{N_{\beta_{S_{1}}}},\ \ \underbrace{\sqrt{\tfrac{\Delta_{n}}{n}},\ldots,\sqrt{\tfrac{\Delta_{n}}{n}}}_{N_{\beta_{S_{2}}}}\Bigr]^{\top}\Bigr).

Convergence (
44
) is immediately deduced from the following result.

Lemma 10
.

Assume that conditions
(H)
-II and (
C1
)–(
C5
) hold. If
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, then

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{S,n}(\beta^{\dagger}_{S},\beta_{R},\sigma)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\mathbf{0}_{N_{\beta_{S}}};

(47)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{S,n}\bigl(\hat{\beta}_{S,n},\beta_{R},\sigma\bigr)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}2\times\mathrm{diag}\Bigl(\mathscr{B}_{S_{1}S_{1}}(\beta^{\dagger}_{S},\beta_{R},\sigma),\,\mathscr{B}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(\beta^{\dagger}_{S},\beta_{R},\sigma)\Bigr),

(48)

uniformly in
\displaystyle(\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta_{\beta_{R}}\times\Theta_{\sigma}
, where
\displaystyle\beta^{\dagger}_{S}\equiv(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{2}})
,
\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{S,n}\equiv(\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n},\hat{\beta}_{S_{2},n})
and we have set:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{S_{1}S_{1}}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=720\int\partial_{\beta_{S_{1}}}\bigl(V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\bigr)^{\top}a_{S_{1}}^{-1}(x,\theta)\,\partial_{\beta_{S_{1}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\,\nu_{\theta^{\dagger}}(dx);

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=12\int\partial_{\beta_{S_{2}}}\bigl(V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})\bigr)^{\top}a_{S_{2}}^{-1}(x,\theta)\partial_{\beta_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})\,\nu_{\theta^{\dagger}}(dx),

for
\displaystyle x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\,\theta=(\beta_{S},\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta
, where
\displaystyle\beta_{S}=(\beta_{S_{1}},\beta_{S_{2}})
.

We give the proof in Appendix
D.4
.

C.1.3
Step 3

Finally, we prove the consistency of estimators
\displaystyle(\hat{\beta}_{R,n},\hat{\sigma}_{n})
. Working with the rates of convergence (
43
) and (
44
), we obtain the following result leading to the consistency of
\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{n}
:

Lemma 11
.

Assume that conditions
(H)
-II and (
C1
)–(
C5
) hold. If
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, then

\displaystyle\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{n}\,\ell_{n}\bigl(\hat{\beta}_{S,n},\beta_{R},\sigma\bigr)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\int\Bigl\{\mathrm{tr}\bigl(\Lambda(x,(\beta^{\dagger}_{S},\sigma))\Sigma(x,(\beta^{\dagger}_{S},\sigma^{\dagger}))\bigr)+\log\bigl|\Sigma(x,(\beta^{\dagger}_{S},\sigma))\bigr|\Bigr\}\nu_{\theta^{\dagger}}(dx),

uniformly in
\displaystyle(\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta_{\beta_{R}}\times\Theta_{\sigma}
.

We provide the proof in Appendix
D.5
.
To show the consistency of
\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{R,n}
, we consider, for

\displaystyle\beta_{R}\in\Theta_{\beta_{R}}
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{L}(\beta_{R}):=\tfrac{1}{n\Delta_{n}}\ell_{n}(\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n},\hat{\beta}_{S_{2},n},\beta_{R},\hat{\sigma}_{n})-\tfrac{1}{n\Delta_{n}}\ell_{n}(\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n},\hat{\beta}_{S_{2},n},\beta^{\dagger}_{R},\hat{\sigma}_{n}).

The consistency of estimator
\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{R,n}
is obtained via the following result whose proof is given in Appendix
D.6
.

Lemma 12
.

Assume that conditions
(H)
-II and (
C1
)–(
C5
) hold. If
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, then

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{L}(\beta_{R})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\int\eta_{R}(x,\beta_{R})^{\top}a_{R}^{-1}\bigl(x,\sigma^{\dagger}\bigr)\,\eta_{R}(x,\beta_{R})\,\nu_{\theta^{\dagger}}(dx),

uniformly in
\displaystyle\beta_{R}\in\Theta_{\beta_{R}}
.

The proof of consistency for the contrast estimator
\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{n}
is now complete.

C.2
Proof of Theorem
2
– Asymptotic Normality

We consider the Taylor expansion of the contrast function

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\theta)
:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{n}(\theta^{\dagger})=\int_{0}^{1}\mathscr{I}_{n}\bigl(\theta^{\dagger}+\lambda(\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta^{\dagger})\bigr)d\lambda\times{M}_{n}(\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta^{\dagger})

where we have set, for
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{n}(\theta)=-M_{n}^{-1}\,\partial_{\theta}\ell_{n}(\theta),\quad\mathscr{I}_{n}(\theta)=M_{n}^{-1}\,\partial^{2}_{\theta}\ell_{n}(\theta)\,M_{n}^{-1},\quad M_{n}=\mathrm{diag}({v}_{n}),

with the
\displaystyle N_{\theta}
-dimensional vector
\displaystyle{v}_{n}
defined as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle v_{n}=\Bigl[\,\underbrace{\sqrt{\tfrac{n}{\Delta_{n}^{3}}},\ldots,\sqrt{\tfrac{n}{\Delta_{n}^{3}}}}_{N_{\beta_{S_{1}}}},\ \ \underbrace{\sqrt{\tfrac{n}{\Delta_{n}}},\ldots,\sqrt{\tfrac{n}{\Delta_{n}}}}_{N_{\beta_{S_{2}}}},\ \ \underbrace{\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}},\ldots,\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}}}_{N_{\beta_{R}}},\ \ \underbrace{\sqrt{n},\ldots,\sqrt{n}}_{N_{\sigma}}\,\Bigr]^{\top}.

The asymptotic normality immediately holds from the following two results – their proofs are shown in Appendices
D.7
and
D.8
.

Lemma 13
.

Assume that conditions
(H)
-II and (
C1
)–(
C5
) hold.
If
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, then

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{n}\bigl(\theta^{\dagger}+\lambda(\hat{\theta}_{n}-\theta^{\dagger})\bigr)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}2\Gamma(\theta^{\dagger}),

uniformly in
\displaystyle\lambda\in[0,1]
, where the matrix
\displaystyle\Gamma(\theta^{\dagger})
is defined as in (
15
) in the main text.

Lemma 14
.

Assume that conditions
(H)
-II and (
C1
)–(
C5
) hold.
If
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, with
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}=o(n^{-1/2})
, then

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{n}(\theta^{\dagger})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{L}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\mathscr{N}\bigl(\mathbf{0}_{N_{\theta}},4\Gamma(\theta^{\dagger})\bigr).

The proof of Theorem
2
is now complete.

Appendix D
Proof of Technical Results

D.1
Proof of Lemma
7

We have that
\displaystyle\textstyle\tfrac{\Delta_{n}^{3}}{n}\ell_{n}(\theta)=\sum_{1\leq i\leq 4}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\theta)
,
\displaystyle\theta=(\beta_{S_{1}},\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta
, where we have set:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{1}(\theta)=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{S_{1}}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}})^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\,\eta_{S_{1}}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}});

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{2}(\theta)=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq N}R_{1}^{j_{1}j_{2}}(\Delta_{n}^{q_{1}},X_{{i-1}},\theta)\,m_{i}^{j_{1}}(\Delta,\theta^{\dagger})\,m_{i}^{j_{2}}(\Delta,\theta^{\dagger});

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{3}(\theta)=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{1\leq j\leq N}R_{2}^{j}(\Delta_{n}^{q_{2}},X_{{i-1}},\theta)\,m_{i}^{j}(\Delta,\theta^{\dagger});

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{4}(\theta)=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}R_{3}(\Delta_{n}^{q_{3}},X_{{i-1}},\theta),

for some functions
\displaystyle R_{1}^{j_{1}j_{2}},R_{2}^{j},R_{3}\in\mathcal{S}
and constants
\displaystyle q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}\geq 1
. From Lemmas
2
,
3
, we immediately have that as
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{1}(\theta)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\int\eta_{S_{1}}(x,\beta_{S_{1}})^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)\,\eta_{S_{1}}(x,\beta_{S_{1}})\nu_{\theta^{\dagger}}(dx),\qquad\mathscr{E}_{k}(\theta)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}0,\ \ 2\leq k\leq 4,

uniformly in
\displaystyle\theta=(\beta_{S_{1}},\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta
, and the proof is now complete.

D.2
Proof of Lemma
8

We define
\displaystyle\mathscr{F}:\Theta\to\mathbb{R}^{N_{\beta_{S_{1}}}\times N_{\beta_{S_{1}}}}
as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{F}(\theta)=\tfrac{\Delta_{n}^{3}}{n}\partial_{\beta_{S_{1}}}^{2}\ell_{n}\left(\theta\right),\quad\theta\in\Theta.

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}(\theta)
can be expressed as
\displaystyle\textstyle{\mathscr{F}(\theta)=\sum_{1\leq k\leq 6}\mathscr{F}_{k}(\theta)}
, where we have set, for
\displaystyle 1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq N_{\beta_{S_{1}}}
with multi-index
\displaystyle\mathbf{j}=(j_{1},j_{2})
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle[\mathscr{F}_{1}(\theta)]_{j_{1}j_{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl(\partial^{\beta_{S_{1}}}_{j_{1}}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{S,i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}})\bigr)^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\partial^{\beta_{S_{1}}}_{j_{2}}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{S,i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}});

\displaystyle\displaystyle[\mathscr{F}_{2}(\theta)]_{j_{1}j_{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{S_{1}}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}})^{\top}\partial_{\mathbf{j}}^{\beta_{S_{1}}}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\,\eta_{S_{1}}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}});

\displaystyle\displaystyle[\mathscr{F}_{3}(\theta)]_{j_{1}j_{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=-\tfrac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{S_{1}}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}})^{\top}\partial_{\mathbf{j}}^{\beta_{S_{1}}}\bigl\{\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(X_{{i-1}},\theta)V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{S,i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}})\bigr\};

\displaystyle\displaystyle[\mathscr{F}_{4}(\theta)]_{j_{1}j_{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{1\leq k_{1},k_{2}\leq N}R_{k_{1}k_{2}}^{j_{1}j_{2}}(\Delta_{n}^{q_{1}},X_{{i-1}},\theta)\,m_{i}^{k_{1}}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger})\,m_{i}^{k_{2}}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger});

\displaystyle\displaystyle[\mathscr{F}_{5}(\theta)]_{j_{1}j_{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{1\leq k\leq N}R_{k}^{j_{1}j_{2}}(\Delta_{n}^{q_{2}},X_{{i-1}},\theta)\,m_{i}^{k}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger});

\displaystyle\displaystyle[\mathscr{F}_{6}(\theta)]_{j_{1}j_{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}R^{j_{1}j_{2}}(\Delta_{n}^{q_{3}},X_{{i-1}},\theta),

for some functions
\displaystyle R^{j_{1}j_{2}}_{k_{1}k_{2}},R^{j_{1}j_{2}}_{k},R^{j_{1}j_{2}}\in\mathcal{S}
and constants
\displaystyle q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}>0
. It follows from Lemmas
2
,
3
and the consistency of estimator
\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}
that if
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Bigl[\mathscr{F}_{1}\bigl(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}}-\lambda(\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}-\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}}),\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma\bigr)\Bigr]_{j_{1}j_{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}2\int\bigl(\partial^{\beta_{S_{1}}}_{j_{1}}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}})\bigr)^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}\bigl(x,(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},\beta_{S_{2}},\sigma)\bigr)\partial^{\beta_{S_{1}}}_{j_{2}}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}})\nu_{\theta^{\dagger}}(dx);

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Bigl[\mathscr{F}_{k}\bigl(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}}-\lambda(\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}-\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}}),\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma\bigr)\Bigr]_{j_{1}j_{2}}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}0,\qquad 2\leq k\leq 6,

uniformly in
\displaystyle(\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta_{\beta_{S_{2}}}\times\Theta_{\beta_{R}}\times\Theta_{\sigma}
and
\displaystyle\lambda\in[0,1]
. The proof is now complete.

D.3
Proof of Lemma
9

We write
\displaystyle\theta^{-S_{1}}\equiv\bigl(\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma\bigr)\in\Theta_{\beta_{S_{2}}}\times\Theta_{\beta_{R}}\times\Theta_{\sigma}
. It holds that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\tfrac{\Delta_{n}}{n}\,\ell_{n}(\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n},\,\theta^{-S_{1}})=\sum_{1\leq k\leq 7}\mathscr{G}_{k}(\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n},\,\theta^{-S_{1}}),

where we have set, for
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{1}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{\Delta_{n}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{S_{1},i-1}(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}},\beta_{S_{1}})^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\eta_{S_{1},i-1}(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}},\beta_{S_{1}});

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{2}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{1\leq j\leq N}R_{j}(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}},X_{{i-1}},\theta)\eta_{S_{1},i-1}^{j}(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}},\beta_{S_{1}});

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{3}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq N}R_{j_{1}j_{2}}({\Delta_{n}},X_{{i-1}},\theta)\eta_{S_{1},i-1}^{j_{1}}(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}},\beta_{S_{1}})m_{i}^{j_{2}}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger});

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{4}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Biggl\{\tfrac{1}{4}\Bigl(\mathcal{L}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{i-1}},\theta^{\dagger})-\mathcal{L}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\Bigr)^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\,\Bigl(\mathcal{L}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{i-1}},\theta^{\dagger})-\mathcal{L}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\tfrac{1}{2}\Bigl(\mathcal{L}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{i-1}},\theta^{\dagger})-\mathcal{L}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\Bigr)^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{2}}(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\,\eta_{S_{2}}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{2}})

\displaystyle\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\tfrac{1}{2}\eta_{S_{2}}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{2}})^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{2}S_{1}}(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\,\Bigl(\mathcal{L}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{i-1}},\theta^{\dagger})-\mathcal{L}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\eta_{S_{2}}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{2}})^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{2}S_{2}}(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\eta_{S_{2}}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{2}})\Biggr\};

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{5}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{1\leq j\leq N}\widetilde{R}_{j}({\Delta_{n}},X_{{i-1}},\theta)\,m_{i}^{j}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger});

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{6}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{\Delta_{n}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigl\{m_{i}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger})^{\top}\Lambda(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\,m_{i}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger})+\log\bigl|\Sigma(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\bigr|\Bigr\},

for some functions
\displaystyle R_{j},R_{j_{1}j_{2}},\,\widetilde{R}_{j}\in\mathcal{S}
. Note that
\displaystyle\eta_{S_{1},i-1}^{j}(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}},\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n})
can be expressed as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\eta_{S_{1},i-1}^{j}(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}},\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n})=\sum_{1\leq k\leq N_{\beta_{S_{1}}}}\tfrac{\eta_{S_{1}}^{j,[k]}(X_{{S,i-1}})}{|\beta_{S_{1}}^{\dagger,k}-\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}^{k}|}\times\Bigl|\tfrac{\beta_{S_{1}}^{\dagger,k}-\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}^{k}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}}}\Bigr|,

where we have set:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\eta_{S_{1}}^{j,[k]}(X_{{S,i-1}})\equiv V_{S_{1},0}^{j}(X_{{S,i-1}},\bar{\beta}_{S_{1},n}^{[k-1]})-V_{S_{1},0}^{j}(X_{{S,i-1}},\bar{\beta}_{S_{1},n}^{[k]}),\quad 1\leq k\leq N_{\beta_{S_{1}}},

with the notation:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bar{\beta}_{S_{1},n}^{[\ell]}=(\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}^{1},\ldots,\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}^{\ell},{\beta}_{S_{1}}^{\dagger,\ell+1},\ldots,{\beta}_{S_{1}}^{\dagger,N_{\beta_{S_{1}}}}),\quad 1\leq\ell\leq N_{\beta_{S_{1}}}-1;

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bar{\beta}_{S_{1},n}^{[0]}=\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},\quad\bar{\beta}_{S_{1},n}^{\,[N_{\beta_{S_{1}}}]}=\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}.

From convergence (
43
), Lemma
2
and condition (
C2
) it follows that if
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, then

\displaystyle\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\eta_{S_{1},i-1}^{j}(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}},\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}0,\end{aligned}

(49)

uniformly in
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta=(\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta_{\beta_{S_{2}}}\times\Theta_{\beta_{R}}\times\Theta_{\sigma}
for any
\displaystyle f:\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying the same property in Lemma
2
.
Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{k}(\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n},\theta^{-S_{1}})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}0,\qquad 1\leq k\leq 3,

uniformly in
\displaystyle\theta^{-S_{1}}
. Also, we immediately have from Lemmas
2
,
3
that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{k}(\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n},\theta^{-S_{1}})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}0,\qquad k=5,6,

as
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, uniformly in
\displaystyle\theta^{-S_{1}}
. Finally, we consider the fourth term
\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{4}
. Noticing that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{L}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{i-1}},\theta^{\dagger})-\mathcal{L}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{i-1}},\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad=\partial_{x_{S_{1}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{S,i-1}},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}})\,\eta_{S_{1}}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}})+\Bigl(\partial_{x_{S_{1}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{S,i-1}},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}})-\partial_{x_{S_{1}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{S,i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}})\Bigr)V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{S,i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}})

\displaystyle\displaystyle\qquad+\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{S,i-1}},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}})\,\eta_{S_{2}}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{2}})+\Bigl(\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{S,i-1}},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}})-\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{S,i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}})\Bigr)V_{S_{2},0}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{2}}),

we obtain from Lemmas
2
,
3
and consistency of
\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n}
that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{4}(\hat{\beta}_{S_{1},n},\theta^{-S_{1}})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\int\eta_{S_{2}}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})^{\top}\,\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}_{4}\bigl(x,(\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}},\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma)\bigr)\,\eta_{S_{2}}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})\,\nu_{\theta^{\dagger}}(dx),

as
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, uniformly in
\displaystyle\theta^{-S_{1}}
, where we have set: for
\displaystyle x=(x_{S},x_{R})\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{S}}\times\mathbb{R}^{N_{R}}
and
\displaystyle\theta=(\beta_{S_{1}},\beta_{S_{2}},\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}_{4}\bigl(x,\theta\bigr)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{4}\bigl(\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\bigr)^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}(x,\theta)\,\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl(\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})\bigr)^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{2}}(x,\theta)+\tfrac{1}{2}\Lambda_{S_{2}S_{1}}(x,\theta)\,\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})+\Lambda_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta).

Lemmas
4
,
5
yield:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{G}}_{4}\bigl(x,\theta\bigr)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\Lambda_{S_{2}S_{1}}(x,\theta)\,\partial_{x_{S_{2}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta_{S_{1}})+\Lambda_{S_{2}S_{2}}(x,\theta)=12a_{S_{2}}^{-1}(x,\theta).

The proof is now complete.

D.4
Proof of Lemma
10

(
Proof of (
47
)
).
Since the proof of
\displaystyle\tfrac{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{3}}}{n}\partial_{\beta_{S_{1}}}\ell_{n}(\beta^{\dagger}_{S},\beta_{R},\sigma)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\mathbf{0}_{N_{\beta_{S_{1}}}}
is identical with that in Section
C.1.1
, we will only show that, if
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, then

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{K}(\beta^{\dagger}_{S},\beta_{R},\sigma):=\tfrac{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}}}{n}\partial_{\beta_{S_{2}}}\ell_{n}(\beta^{\dagger}_{S},\beta_{R},\sigma)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\mathbf{0}_{N_{\beta_{S_{2}}}},

(50)

uniformly in
\displaystyle(\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta_{\beta_{R}}\times\Theta_{\sigma}
. It holds that

\displaystyle\textstyle\mathscr{K}(\beta^{\dagger}_{S},\beta_{R},\sigma)=\sum_{1\leq l\leq 3}\mathscr{K}_{l}(\beta^{\dagger}_{S},\beta_{R},\sigma)
,
where we have set, for
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{K}_{1}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{1\leq j\leq N}R_{j}(1,X_{{i-1}},\theta)\,m_{i}^{j}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger});

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{K}_{2}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq N}R_{j_{1}j_{2}}(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}},X_{{i-1}},\theta)\,m_{i}^{j_{1}}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger})\,m_{i}^{j_{2}}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger});

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathscr{K}_{3}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}R(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}},X_{{i-1}},\theta),

for
\displaystyle R,R_{j},R_{j_{1}j_{2}}\in\mathcal{S}
. From Lemmas
2
and
3
, we immediately have that
\displaystyle\mathscr{K}_{i}(\beta^{\dagger}_{S},\beta_{R},\sigma)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}\mathbf{0}_{N_{\beta_{S_{2}}}},\;1\leq i\leq 3
, as
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, thus (
50
) holds.

(

)
).
We define

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}(\theta)=M_{\beta_{S},n}\,\partial^{2}_{\beta_{S}}\ell_{n}(\theta)\,M_{\beta_{S},n}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathcal{Q}_{S_{1}S_{1}}(\theta)&\mathcal{Q}_{S_{1}S_{2}}(\theta)\\[5.69046pt]
\mathcal{Q}_{S_{2}S_{1}}(\theta)&\mathcal{Q}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(\theta)\end{bmatrix},

where we have set:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{S_{1}S_{1}}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{\Delta_{n}^{3}}{n}\partial^{2}_{\beta_{S_{1}}}\ell_{n}(\theta),\quad\mathcal{Q}_{S_{1}S_{2}}(\theta)=\tfrac{\Delta_{n}^{2}}{n}\partial_{\beta_{S_{1}}}\partial_{\beta_{S_{2}}}^{\top}\ell_{n}(\theta);

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{S_{2}S_{1}}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\mathcal{Q}_{S_{1}S_{2}}(\theta)^{\top},\quad\mathcal{Q}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(\theta)=\tfrac{\Delta_{n}}{n}\partial^{2}_{\beta_{S_{2}}}\ell_{n}(\theta),

for
\displaystyle\theta=(\beta_{S},\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta
. From the proof of Lemma
8
in Appendix
D.2
and the consistency of the estimator
\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{S,n}
, we have that if
\displaystyle n\to\infty
,
\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\to 0
and
\displaystyle n\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, then

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{S_{1}S_{1}}\bigl(\beta^{\dagger}_{S}+\lambda(\hat{\beta}_{S,n}-\beta^{\dagger}_{S}),\beta_{R},\sigma\bigr)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{\dagger}}}2\int\partial_{\beta_{S_{1}}}\bigl(V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}})\bigr)^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}\bigl(x,(\beta^{\dagger}_{S},\sigma)\bigr)\partial_{\beta_{S_{1}}}^{\top}V_{S_{1},0}(x_{S},\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{1}})\,\nu_{\theta^{\dagger}}(dx),

(51)

uniformly in
\displaystyle(\beta_{R},\sigma)\in\Theta_{\beta_{R}}\times\Theta_{\sigma}
and
\displaystyle\lambda\in[0,1]
.
We will check the convergence of the two matrices
\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{S_{1}S_{2}}(\theta)
and
\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{S_{2}S_{2}}(\theta)
.

We have

\displaystyle\textstyle{\mathcal{Q}_{S_{1}S_{2}}(\theta)=\sum_{1\leq k\leq 4}\mathcal{Q}_{S_{1}S_{2},k}(\theta)}
, where we set for
\displaystyle 1\leq j_{1}\leq N_{\beta_{S_{1}}}
,
\displaystyle 1\leq j_{2}\leq N_{\beta_{S_{2}}}
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bigl[\mathcal{Q}_{S_{1}S_{2},1}(\theta)\bigr]_{j_{1}j_{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl(\partial^{\beta_{S_{1}}}_{j_{1}}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{S,i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}})\bigr)^{\top}\times\biggl\{\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{2}}\bigl(X_{{i-1}},\theta\bigr)\partial^{\beta_{S_{2}}}_{j_{2}}V_{S_{2},0}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{2}})

\displaystyle\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\tfrac{1}{2}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}\bigl(X_{{i-1}},\theta\bigr)\partial^{\beta_{S_{2}}}_{j_{2}}\mathcal{L}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S})\biggr\};

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bigl[\mathcal{Q}_{S_{1}S_{2},2}(\theta)\bigr]_{j_{1}j_{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k_{1},k_{2}\leq N_{S_{1}}\end{subarray}}R_{j_{1}j_{2}}^{k_{1}k_{2}}(1,X_{{i-1}},\theta)\eta_{S_{1},i-1}^{k_{1}}(\Delta_{n},\beta_{S_{1}})\eta_{S_{1}}^{k_{2}}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}});

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bigl[\mathcal{Q}_{S_{1}S_{2},3}(\theta)\bigr]_{j_{1}j_{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k_{1}\leq N_{S_{1}}\\
1\leq k_{2}\leq N_{S}\end{subarray}}\widetilde{R}_{j_{1}j_{2}}^{k_{1}k_{2}}(1,X_{{i-1}},\theta)\eta_{S_{1}}^{k_{1}}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{1}})b^{k_{2}}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S});

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bigl[\mathcal{Q}_{S_{1}S_{2},4}(\theta)\bigr]_{j_{1}j_{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\biggl\{\sum_{1\leq k_{1},k_{2}\leq N}\bar{R}_{j_{1}j_{2}}^{k_{1}k_{2}}(\Delta_{n},X_{{i-1}},\theta)m_{i}^{k_{1}}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger})m_{i}^{k_{2}}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\sum_{1\leq k\leq N}\bar{R}_{j_{1}j_{2}}^{k}(\Delta_{n},X_{{i-1}},\theta)m_{i}^{k}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger})+\bar{R}(\Delta_{n},X_{{i-1}},\theta)\biggr\},

for some functions
\displaystyle R_{j_{1}j_{2}}^{k_{1}k_{2}},\,\widetilde{R}_{j_{1}j_{2}}^{k_{1}k_{2}},\,\bar{R}_{j_{1}j_{2}}^{k_{1}k_{2}},\,\bar{R}_{j_{1}j_{2}}^{k},\,\bar{R}\in\mathcal{S}
, and the function

is defined as:

V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})-V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta^{\dagger}_{S_{2}})\end{bmatrix},

Notice that for any
\displaystyle\theta\in\Theta
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle[\mathcal{Q}_{S_{1}S_{2},1}(\theta)]_{j_{1}j_{2}}=0,\,\quad 1\leq j_{1}\leq N_{\beta_{S_{1}}},\quad 1\leq j_{2}\leq N_{\beta_{S_{2}}},

because it follows from Lemma
4

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{2}}\bigl(x,\theta\bigr)\partial^{\beta_{S_{2}}}_{j_{2}}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})+\tfrac{1}{2}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{1}}\bigl(x,\theta\bigr)\partial^{\beta_{S_{2}}}_{j_{2}}\mathcal{L}V_{S_{1},0}(x,\beta_{S})=\Phi(x,\theta)\,\partial^{\beta_{S_{2}}}_{j_{2}}V_{S_{2},0}(x,\beta_{S_{2}})=\mathbf{0}_{N_{S_{1}}},

\displaystyle x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
,

,

).
From Lemmas
2
,
3

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{S,n}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{1\leq k_{1},k_{2}\leq N_{S_{1}}}R_{j_{1}j_{2}}^{k_{1}k_{2}}(1,X_{{i-1}},\theta)\eta_{S_{1},i-1}^{k_{1}}(\Delta_{n},\beta_{S_{1}})\eta_{S_{1},i-1}^{k_{2}}(\Delta_{n},\beta_{S_{1}})

1\leq k_{2}\leq N\end{subarray}}\widetilde{R}_{j_{1}j_{2}}^{k_{1}k_{2}}(1,X_{{i-1}},\theta)\eta_{S_{1},i-1}^{k_{1}}(\sqrt{\Delta_{n}},\beta_{S_{1}})m_{i}^{k_{2}}(\Delta_{n},\theta^{\dagger})\biggr\};

\displaystyle\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl(\partial_{j_{1}}^{\beta_{S_{2}}}\mathcal{L}V_{S_{1},0}(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\bigr)^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{1}S_{2}}(X_{{i-1}},\theta)\,\partial_{j_{2}}^{\beta_{S_{2}}}V_{S_{2},0}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{2}})

\displaystyle\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\bigl(\partial^{\beta_{S_{2}}}_{j_{1}}V_{S_{2},0}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{2}})\bigr)^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{2}S_{2}}\bigl(X_{{i-1}},\theta\bigr)\,\partial^{\beta_{S_{2}}}_{j_{2}}V_{S_{2},0}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{2}})\Bigr\};

.

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\tfrac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl(\partial^{\beta_{S_{2}}}_{j_{1}}V_{S_{2},0}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{2}})\bigr)^{\top}\Lambda_{S_{2}S_{2}}\bigl(X_{{i-1}},\theta\bigr)\,\partial^{\beta_{S_{2}}}_{j_{2}}V_{S_{2},0}(X_{{i-1}},\beta_{S_{2}}).
